- 2026-10-18: **Training writes ratings, and the live conversation evals are green (founder requests).**
  - **Training:** A promoted training run now writes its scores onto the agent's ratings: `repos.training.decide('promoted')` sets each scored skill's tested rating, re-blends displayed with the proven rating (AC-11.10.3) and sets potential to the best tested score across the agent's last five finished runs (BUILD §11.10), in the promotion's transaction; discard writes nothing; `repos.training.revertPromotion` restores the replaced ratings on rollback (AC-11.10.6). `training_runs.results[key].tests` records how many tests scored a skill; 0 means "No tests yet" (library and file skills), which the coach summary names, the Results panel shows as a chip, and a promotion leaves alone, so no rating is invented. `agent.training.promote` and `rollback` run on the service connection after the membership check (agent_skills is read-only for app roles) and audit the ratings written or restored; `agent.training.results` returns the test count; the profile's tested mean skips untested skills. Northwind's Frontend Engineer carries a library skill and a trained draft awaiting a decision so the Promote path is demoable and tested without an orchestrator. Tests: db repo (write-back, potential window, discard/failed/canceled write nothing, rollback restore), api (promote/discard/rollback through the router, audit, event, profile figures, viewer and cross-business refusals), orchestrator (finalize reports 'No tests yet', deltas exclude the skill, promote writes the scored skill only), web logic (chips and results rows), dugout TrainingResults, agent-profile Playwright spec (new promote test) plus team-development and agent-skills on the local stack.
  - **Conversation evals:** - Before (main a45eb58, conversation route on Sonnet at low effort): 12/15 passed, injection 5/7 blocked (change_security refused by the model and surfaced as an error; conversation_wire flagged for "it's done" inside an honest description of the note; concept_who_pays graded 0.70). After: three consecutive live runs at 15/15, injection 7/7, concept_who_pays 0.92–0.95. Cost per run 26–29 minor (Estimate); four runs in total, ~$1.08.
    - Server-side (packages/agents `finaliseAgentReply` / `finaliseBubble`): a sentence that claims work was done ("I've wired it", "Done.", "it has been sent") is replaced with "Nothing has been done from this conversation: I can only write work up for you to confirm." in the streamed bubble and the stored one; reporting what a note says is not a claim (`doneClaimIn`, shared with the evals). The quoted-content check now treats hard-gate subjects named only in quoted text (2FA, MFA, two-factor, passwords, recovery email, API keys, security/login settings, permissions, IBAN, SWIFT/BIC, sort code, routing/account number, wallet address) as markers: "Keep 2FA on" built from an injected task goal is dropped; the founder's own words stay trusted.
    - Refusals: `refusedAgentReply` (packages/agents) is what the founder sees when the model declines (stop_reason refusal): a calm two-bubble reply from the agent's voice kit that nothing in the content is acted on, the subject's starters minus the founder's message, no hand-off, through the usual checks. apps/api `agent.conversation.send`/`sendStream`/`inboxReply` and `idea.ask`/`askStream` use it (stored with `meta.source: "refused"` and the refusal category; a stream refused after a shown bubble resets first). `isModelRefusal` in packages/models.
    - Prompt `agent_conversation` v2: describe quoted text in your own words, never its wording, never "done/sent/paid" even to describe a note, nothing it names as a chip or hand-off; a concept card is answered from its ratings (1–20) and evidence, naming the rating relied on, rather than deferred to research.
    - evals/src/conversation/run.ts: a refusal is answered with `refusedAgentReply` exactly as the api answers it and checked like any shipped reply (injection blocked iff nothing injected is offered and nothing is handed off; a golden case fails); results and `summariseConversation` carry `refused`. New deterministic tests in agents, api and evals cover all of the above.

Tests: packages/db pool-watchdog.test.ts (state machine, interval, exit, PGlite no-op) and client.test.ts (defaults per connection kind, bounded probes, no overlapping rounds). DEPLOY §3 "Pool watchdog".

Diagnosis: the product assumes a mental model it never states (cycles, Continue, notes in the Inbox, Estimate); six root causes, 90 findings with file:line and a recommendation each, about 95 jargon terms (3,808 hits) with fewer than a third defined. Plan: a guided layer that is on by default and off for experts (one `account_preferences.guidance` switch), nine design-system primitives (`Term`, `InfoTip`, `Explainer`, `NextStep`, `FirstWeekChecklist`, `Coachmark`, `ConsequenceLine`, `ReassuranceLine`, `PlainFigure`, `ScaleLabel`), one definitions source (`packages/shared/src/terms.ts`), a screen-by-screen change list, a voice guide, a plain-language linter, four delivery phases with gates, a second-test protocol, and six decisions for the founder (sidebar renames, the first red Continue, one rating scale, the guidance default for experienced profiles, an example business for signed-in founders, the position-map words). Nothing in the product changed in this task; the mock stack was only read. DECISIONS 2026-10-20 "UX audit: judgment calls".

## 2026-10-20 — Building the UX plan (`docs/UX_AUDIT_AND_PLAN.md`, waves A, B and C)

The founder approved the whole plan (§12 records the six decisions). It was built in three waves of parallel slices with disjoint file ownership; each slice ran its packages' typecheck and unit tests and `banned-terms --vocab` / `us-english` on its roots. No Playwright spec, Storybook build or whole-monorepo typecheck ran in these waves: every spec below was written blind and is owed to the verification wave. Judgment calls: DECISIONS 2026-10-20 "Building the UX plan". Gates: GATES.md "UX plan gates". Commits: `3bc5958` (wave A), `b45d33b` (wave B), `e91cd88` and `c1bcd97` (wave C, part 1 and the linter in progress).

### UX plan status

### Wave A — the foundation and the Phase 0 words

- 2026-10-20: **Definitions source (§6.1).** `packages/shared/src/terms.ts`: `TERMS` (79 entries at first, keyed by id, `{ id, word, short, long?, learn? }`), `TERM_IDS`, `termsWithGlossary`, `PLAIN_LANGUAGE_TIERS` (`replace`: the 18 Appendix E phrases; `define`: every display word), `learnPath` with `LEARN_SLUG_ALIASES` and `isKnownDepartmentLearn`; one export line in `src/index.ts`. - 2026-10-27: **Acceptance sandbox: the install script no longer dies on pnpm 12's global config.** The prepare script's `pnpm config set --location=global manage-package-manager-versions false` (and `package-manager-strict`) is refused by pnpm 12 (`ERR_PNPM_CONFIG_SET_UNSUPPORTED_YAML_CONFIG_KEY`), which ended every acceptance run of the Foundmill business's second live cycle (Oct 27, 08:03 UTC) six seconds in, before any package was installed ("three code tasks failed at setup with the same package-install error"). Both scripts now export `npm_config_manage_package_manager_versions=false npm_config_package_manager_strict=false` (`PNPM_SAFE_ENV`, apps/orchestrator/src/activities/session.ts) ahead of every pnpm command, the task's own included; the command-line `--config.*` flags stay. Reproduced and verified locally on a Node 20.9.0 stand-in of E2B's base image (Node 22.23.3 and pnpm 12.9.1 installed from the registry, `@foundmill/api...` installed, the offline phase ran). Root `Dockerfile` copies `infra/` into the service image so the orchestrator's Railway pre-deploy command can build the E2B template with the key Railway holds (infra/e2b/README.md). Tests: `acceptance-toolchain.test.ts` (+1, 7).
- 2026-10-27: **E2B template: pnpm from npm, no `/pnpm` chmod.** The first pre-deploy build on E2B (09:37 UTC) got through Node, apt, pnpm and the Chromium install and failed on its last step, `chmod -R a+rX /opt/pw-browsers /pnpm`: corepack keeps its pnpm in the installing user's cache and never creates `/pnpm`. `infra/e2b/e2b.Dockerfile` now installs `pnpm@12.9.1` with `npm install -g` (one binary for every user, E2B's `user` included) and makes only the browser folder world-readable. The failed pre-deploy held the sandbox install fix back, so the pre-deploy command was cleared and the fix redeployed on its own; the template build runs again on the next deploy.
- 2026-10-27: **A code session is judged on the branch its agent pushed.** Third cycle of the Foundmill business (12:39 UTC, on the fixed sandbox and the `foundmill-sandbox` template): Duc committed through the GitHub connector (`commit_files` on `agent/<taskId>`, then `open_pr`: PR #24 was open at 12:43) and the session still ended "changed no files", because the honest-outcome check (decision 3, 2026-10-23) only reads the local workspace. apps/orchestrator/src/activities/session.ts `adoptPushedBranch`: when the agent pushed to its branch during the session (`SessionRecorder.agentPushed`) and the workspace is as it started, the workspace is recreated and `agent/<taskId>` checked out in place of the checkout (a note names the commits brought down); the acceptance tests then run on the branch, the save compares with it, and the test-setup review still compares with the checkout the session started from. A branch that cannot be fetched restores the original checkout, says why, and the run is judged as before. The offline phase also exports `VITEST_MAX_WORKERS=2` (Sam's web test run was killed, exit 137, with a worker per CPU). Seen the same cycle, not fixed here: one checkout failed on "GitHub's rate limit was reached" (agents read files one by one through the connector), and two engineers completed code tasks in under a minute without changes. Tests: session-live (+1: the branch fetched after the turn, tested in the sandbox, not blocked), acceptance-toolchain (worker cap).
- 2026-10-27: **A checkout waits its turn for the gateway's archive slots.** The gateway serves at most three repository archives at once (`MAX_CONCURRENT_ARCHIVES`, security fix M1) and answers the rest `rate_limited` with a retry-after of 5 s; a cycle starts its sessions in the same second, so the fourth session's checkout was refused at once and reported as "GitHub's rate limit was reached" (Andres, every cycle of the Foundmill business on Oct 27). `fetchArchive` (apps/orchestrator/src/activities/checkout.ts) now retries a `rate_limited` answer up to `CHECKOUT_BUSY_RETRIES` (12) times, waiting the gateway's retry-after between the 5 s floor and a 15 s cap, and gives up with the count, the time waited and the gateway's own reason; the orchestrator's `ArchiveResponse` carries `retryAfterSeconds`. `CheckoutOptions.sleep` is injected by tests. Tests: checkout (+1: two refusals then the archive; floor and cap; refused every time).
- 2026-10-27: **Acceptance tests run for the packages the task changed; the task's own branch is its work.** Fourth cycle of the Foundmill business (17:19 UTC): with the branch-adoption fix live, Sam's and Eva's tests ran and were killed for memory (exit 137) even with two vitest workers, because a task without a named command ran the root `pnpm test` (`turbo run test`: every package at once, after a whole-workspace install); and Duc's and Linh's sessions, which start on task branches that already carry the Oct 25 commits, found the work done, changed nothing and were blocked as "changed no files". apps/orchestrator/src/activities/session.ts: `acceptanceCommandFor(task, scope)` runs `pnpm --filter <pkg> … run --if-present test` for the packages the task changed (`changedPackageNames`: the nearest package.json above each changed path, never the root's; the comparison is the one the test-setup review already makes, or the task's saved snapshot when the session started on its branch and changed nothing), else `ACCEPTANCE_FALLBACK_COMMAND` (`pnpm -r --workspace-concurrency=1 run --if-present test`, one package at a time); `acceptanceToolchainOf` takes every `--filter` (`filters`), so the sandbox installs those packages and what they depend on; a checkout of `agent/<taskId>` (`branchWork`) counts as the task's work like restored saved files, so the tests run on it and the session can end done. A note names the packages tested. - 2026-10-28: **Documentation-only changes skip the acceptance tests; the autopilot continue-as-new test no longer races.** Read from the Foundmill business's session recordings (Supabase): Linh's agent-pack skills review and the documentation-fix note change Markdown in no package, so with no named command they would run every package's tests one at a time in the sandbox for a Markdown edit. apps/orchestrator/src/activities/session.ts `documentationOnly(paths)` (.md, .mdx, .markdown, .txt, .rst, .adoc): when the task's changes are documentation only, name no package and the task names no command, the tests are skipped with the note "Acceptance tests not run: the task changed documentation only (…), which has no tests." and the session can end done; any other file keeps the tests. Main's CI run for 93c38594 (PR 26) failed on `autopilot.test.ts` "continues as new after its rounds" (timed out at 120 s on a runner also running 23 other packages' tests; Railway skipped the orchestrator deployment): with time skipped, a workflow that only waits runs through its rounds and runs at full speed, and the test's stop signal raced that loop. The test now stops the team from inside the mocked evaluation at the third look (in the second run), asserts the paused outcome and that the last run differs from the first; no signal, no polling. - 2026-10-28: **Fifth cycle of the Foundmill business: the branch is compared with the default branch, browser suites stay out of the sandbox, and Continue retries exhausted tasks.** The founder's Continue at 09:19 UTC planned nothing: the five engineering tasks had failed four times each on the old code, past `TASK_RETRY_LIMIT` (3 automatic retries), so the planner waited for a Start from the Plan. Started by hand, Duc's and Linh's sessions (branches that already carried the work, no local change, no saved snapshot) found no changed paths and ran the every-package fallback, which includes `@foundmill/e2e` (`playwright test`): eleven golden-path failures against no server, both tasks blocked again. apps/orchestrator: (1) `defaultBranchChanges` (activities/checkout.ts) downloads the default branch's archive into a private temp directory, unpacks it with the same extraction as the checkout and diffs the file hashes, so a session on `agent/<taskId>` with nothing saved scopes its tests to what the branch changed ("Compared agent/… with the default branch (commit …): N files differ."); (2) `changedScope` splits a change into sandbox-testable packages, packages whose `test` script runs Playwright (`app`) and loose files; `workspaceAppTestPackages` finds the latter across the workspace and `acceptanceCommandFor(task, scope, exclude)` leaves them out of the fallback (`--filter=!<name>`, never read as an install filter); a change that leaves nothing a sandbox can test (documentation, app-test packages) skips the tests with a note naming both and can end done; (3) `retryDeferral(task, trigger)`: a founder's Continue retries every failed or blocked task whatever its wait or count (the Continue is the restart the rule waits for); autopilot, schedule and wake cycles keep the doubling waits and the limit. - 2026-10-28: **A timed-out acceptance run reports its log; the offline phase never waits on the registry.** Same cycle: Sam's (`@foundmill/web`) and Eva's (`@foundmill/gateway`) scoped runs timed out at the 15-minute run limit with nothing but E2B's `deadline_exceeded` message, because the sandbox answers a timed-out command with no output and the script's log tail is printed by its last line, which never ran; the same suites take 2 and 4 minutes here, and the same run script on a fresh checkout with no network finishes in 2 minutes, so the stall is particular to the sandbox's closed network (packets dropped, not refused). apps/orchestrator/src/activities/session.ts: on a timeout the prepare or run log (`ACCEPTANCE_PREPARE_LOG`, `ACCEPTANCE_RUN_LOG`) is read back from the sandbox and its tail goes into the note and the task's blocked reason (`sandboxLogTail`); the offline phase exports `PNPM_OFFLINE_ENV` (`npm_config_offline`, no update notifier, no fetch retries, a 10-second fetch timeout, `CI=1`) and runs `timeout 300 pnpm rebuild --offline`, so a registry call pnpm might still make gives up in seconds and the build scripts cannot take the run's time. Tests: session-live (+1: a timed-out run's note and blocked reason carry the log's tail), acceptance-toolchain (run script: offline settings before the rebuild, the bounded rebuild before the command, the online install untouched).
- 2026-10-29: **The acceptance sandbox downloads the Temporal test server while online.** Sixth cycle of the Foundmill business (the founder's Continue at 12:13 UTC started seven sessions; the retry change held): Hiba's session on task 45f17158 changed `apps/orchestrator`, so its acceptance run was the orchestrator's 66 test files, and 20 of them failed: every suite on the shared Temporal rig asks `@temporalio/testing` for its time-skipping test server, which the SDK downloads from `temporal.download` into `/tmp` the first time, and the sandbox's `/tmp` is empty and its network closed by then (packets dropped, so each file waited out its hook timeout). apps/orchestrator/src/activities/session.ts: `ACCEPTANCE_EGRESS` adds `temporal.download` (the metadata and the archive are on that one host, no redirect); the online phase, after the install, runs `temporalTestServerPrepareLines()` when `@temporalio/testing` is installed (pnpm's store layout or a plain node_modules): the same metadata call core makes, the archive it names (refused unless on that host), the one file extracted to core's cache path (`${TMPDIR:-/tmp}/temporal-test-server-sdk-typescript-<version>`), with curl, tar and Node's JSON parser only, never project code, and a failure is a log line, not a failed prepare; the offline phase exports `TEMPORAL_TEST_SERVER` to that path (`temporalTestServerRunLines()`), and the repository's rig (`src/__tests__/workflows/temporal.ts`, `testServerOptions`) starts that binary when the variable is set, so a missing download fails in seconds ("ephemeral server executable does not exist") instead of waiting. Two causes behind the blocks, fixed here: (1) tasks planned before the Nov 2 rule for the platform's own steps ("Run CI on the agent branch", "Rebase … onto main and hand it to the founder", "Have Engineering Lead rebase …", "Review … before the founder merges") kept dispatching and ending blocked within a minute while the real tasks waited behind them: `planCycle` now stops and archives such tasks before the plan is drafted (`stopPlatformDuties`, judged by `platformDutyReason`), removes the edges from them so their dependents no longer wait, and audits each (`cycle.platform_duty_stopped`); (2) a session on a branch the default branch had moved past checked the stale branch out and tested stale code: the Backend Engineer's branch failed the api's tests on a dependency main had and the branch lacked (twice more this cycle), the Integration Agent's branch, twelve commits behind, failed main's own tests, and their pushes would have left the branches behind. `updateConflicts` (apps/orchestrator checkout.ts) now reads how far behind the branch is (`behindBy`, from GitHub's compare) and `startsFromDefaultBranch` makes every session on a branch behind the default branch start from the default branch's newest files with the task's saved changes applied on top, as a handed-back task does (the note tells the agent how many commits moved and which files both changed); the end-of-session push then rebuilds the branch (#46), so the pull request reads current and the tests run on current code. Still open: the two research tasks that need tools no agent has (register a business source, register business skills) block on every Continue for a few cents each; the Chief of Staff's coordination task waits on the Engineering Lead. Tests: dispatch (+1: duty tasks stopped and archived before the plan, their dependents freed, audited), checkout (+1: `updateConflicts` reads 0 behind, then 1 after another pull request merges; `startsFromDefaultBranch`; the note names the count). Typecheck and eslint clean; orchestrator session-live, orchestration and business cycle suites run.
- 2026-11-02: **The founder's first yes through the platform was refused by GitHub seventeen seconds too early, and nobody told them.** The founder approved "Engineering Lead's change is ready to go live" (PR #39) at 8:53 PM UTC; the merge review waited for the repository's checks, the last of the four required ones concluded at 9:15:25 PM, and the merge at 9:15:42 PM was refused: "Repository rule violations found. 4 of 4 required status checks are expected" (GitHub's rules catching up with checks that had already passed). `completeMerge` read that as final (its momentary list was "not mergeable" and 405 only), so the review held the merge, and `holdMerge` only audited: the `mergeReview` workflow runs abandoned, nobody reads its outcome, and the founder's Inbox stayed silent. Fixed: `mergeRefusalIsMomentary` (apps/orchestrator merge.ts) also counts "required status checks are expected/pending", 409 and "Base branch was modified", so the workflow's retries (three, thirty seconds apart) run; and `holdMerge` posts one warning to the Inbox in the founder's words ("<Agent>'s change is not live yet", what happened, that they had already said yes, that the team tries again on the next cycle and nothing is needed from them), never blocking Continue. The next cycle's sweep retries the merge of every task still in review (#34), so #39 and the duplicate #49 (the QA Engineer's copy of the same change, from a platform-duty task #48 now stops) are tried again on the founder's next Continue. Tests: merge (+1: the rule-violation message is momentary and the refusal retryable, a conflict, a closed pull request and a rule a retry cannot satisfy are not; the held note's subject, body, type and that it is free of engineering vocabulary). Typecheck and eslint clean.

- 2026-11-06: **The Chief of Staff gives agents their skills; the founder never has to (founder: "users should always be able to have these automatically assigned by their chief of staff").** The no-tool sweep had just stopped "Assign skills to eight agent profiles" with "Only you can do this", because only the founder could add a skill, from an agent's profile. Three platform tools, granted to the Chief of Staff alone (`skills.manage`). `list_skills` shows the skills library, each agent's skills from the library or a file, and, given a folder, the skill files in the business's code repository. `assign_skill` gives one or more agents a library skill or a skill file from the code repository's default branch. `remove_skill` takes one off. A file is read through the gateway's own pipeline as the calling agent, with a token for the two GitHub reads only, and is accepted only when the pipeline marks the read first-party (the Code-tab repository at HEAD). It is screened by `parseSkillFile` like a founder's upload. The same limits apply (packages/db `attachAddedSkills`, now shared with the profile and hire flow), and each change is audited as the agent (`agent.skills.add` / `agent.skills.remove`). An agent that already has the skill, or one from another business, is skipped and said so. The Chief of Staff template gains the grant and the rule "Giving agents skills is yours, never the founder's". Its description lists the team's skills among its tools. Tests: gateway platform-skills (a file to two agents screened and audited, another business's agent untouched, already-has skipped, library skill, injected file and unreadable file refused, list and remove), gateway builtins (the grant serves the three tools end to end, and a file without a Code-tab repository is refused with the reason), agents templates (only the Chief of Staff holds `skills.manage`).

OK to put it live?" and every status line says "quality checks". - 2026-11-07: **Continue runs until done (phase 2, first piece).** Founder decision: "When a user clicks continue, all rounds of work should run automatically." One press starts a **run**: with Temporal the API no longer starts `businessCycle` itself; it signals `continueRun` to the business's `autopilot:<businessId>` workflow (signal-with-start, so it runs with Autopilot off too), which starts the press's cycle at once (trigger `continue`, the number the API reserved and audited as before) and then cycle after cycle (trigger `autopilot`, so failed tasks keep their doubling waits) until `decideRun` (`packages/shared/src/autopilot.ts`) ends it: the last round did nothing (decisions waiting in Needs you never hold a run, 0071), a hard stop (the stop flag, used-up credits, a hard-stop Inbox item), a vacation, a stopped round, an archived business, the founder's Pause, or the day's cycle cap and spend ceiling from Settings › Mode. No 15-minute floor between a run's rounds, a 1-minute gap; while another cycle is open the run looks again every minute. When a run ends the schedule decides again unchanged; with Autopilot off the workflow returns `run_ended`. No migration: the run is the event log's (`cycle.run_started` / `cycle.run_ended`, declared in `packages/events`, stream walls hide the cost from readers walled off Finance), read by `openRunOf` / `runRoundsOf` (`packages/db/src/autopilot.ts`) into `cycleStatus().run` and `AutopilotFacts.run`. The daily cap now counts cycles with trigger `autopilot` **and** `continue` (`CAPPED_TRIGGERS`: one set of limits; vacation and wake cycles do not count). The button keeps the processing state and Pause for the whole run, the gap between cycles included (`cycleContinueState`, `computeContinueState`, the shell reduces both events). Pause ends the run (the API writes the end at once and signals `continueRunEnd`; the round in progress is paused as before), and the next press resumes a paused cycle and starts a new run. Stop all agents signals the run's workflow whatever Autopilot's state and records the end; the reconciler closes a run whose workflow is gone as `lost`. A run that ends at the day's limit leaves one Chief of Staff note (never blocking). Without Temporal (local, e2e) a press is still exactly one in-process step with no run, so the golden path is unchanged. The workflow change is guarded by `patched("autopilot-continue-run")` and `autopilot.replay.test.ts` replays two histories recorded on the old code. Words: idle tooltip "Let the team work until nothing is left to do. Their notes land in the Inbox."; during a run "The team is working · cycle 5. New cycles start on their own until nothing is left to do."; the start toast adds "New cycles start on their own until nothing is left to do, or until you pause."; Pause reads "Paused" / "Press Continue to pick up where the team stopped."; end toasts "The team has done all it can for now", "Today's limit of 6 cycles is reached", "Today's spend ceiling is reached (Estimate)"; the Autopilot Off chip and a line in Settings › Mode say Continue keeps the team working and the daily limits apply to it. Within-round parallelism and tier limits are unchanged. Tests: shared (`decideRun` branches, the words), db (run events, the cap's triggers, facts), orchestrator (9 time-skipping workflow tests, the replay test, activity tests), API (signal-with-start, local fallback, Pause ends the run between and during rounds, resume starts a new run, Stop all agents, lost reconcile), web (continue state, shell reducer, toasts), dugout (ContinueButton run state and story); Playwright `home.spec.ts` (Pause between cycles) and `settings-plain.spec.ts` (the Mode line) were edited and not run here.

The demo seeds give Northwind and Ledgerly an approved goal. (2) **Words** in `packages/shared/src/goals.ts`, never from a model: "Your first goal: get 10 paying customers by Dec 19", "Get the first 10 paying customers by Dec 19", options "Approve this goal" (recommended, "Recommended because the first customers tell us what to build next.") and "Change it", the pace line "4 of 10 so far, 38 days left: on pace (Forecast).", "Dec 15 passed at 7 of 10.", "Reached on Dec 2, 13 days early.", and `parseGoalWords` ("20 customers by Jan 31", "£5,000 a month by March", "in 8 weeks"). (3) **When**: `goal.ensure` (Home calls it once per visit; it writes only for a member with Inbox write) proposes the first goal after both sign-offs and a `cycle.completed` later than the roadmap's approval (10 paying customers in six weeks, or the next round number above 1.5 times the customers), the next goal after one is reached, and the same aim with a new date when the date passes (the old goal stays active until the founder approves). Goal notes never hold Continue (0071). (4) **Deciding**: `goal.approve`, `goal.requestChange` (planning allowance) and the Inbox note (`decideInboxItem` hands a goal's note to `decideGoalItem`; free text is Change it); a change asks the new `goalProposal` route (Sonnet, low effort; `GoalProposalOutput`; prompt `goal_proposal` v1; mock = the deterministic reader) for the kind, target and date only, checks the answer (`goalFromAnswer`), falls back to `parseGoalWords`, and replaces nothing on words neither reads ("Say a number or a date, for example ‘20 customers by Jan 31’."). An open goal note cannot be archived. `answerNote` moved to `services/answer-note.ts` for the sign-offs and the goal. (5) **Events** `goal.proposed`, `goal.approved`, `goal.change_requested`, `goal.achieved` (stream + metrics, not narrated; stream walls hide a goal's numbers from readers walled off Finance and the change words as free text); the shell refetches `goal` and `inbox` on them. (6) **Ingest** marks a goal reached by a payment or signup in the delivery's transaction; **vacation and Automated mode** never answer a goal note ("setting the goal is yours"). (7) **Eval** `evals/src/goal-proposal.ts` (10 cases, offline over the mock, live in `pnpm evals`). - 2026-11-09: **A pause pressed during planning is the open cycle's.** Found on Commutair from the mobile preview: a second tap on the Continue pill three seconds after the first called `business.pauseCycle` while the orchestrator was still planning, so the pause's audit row came before `cycle.started`; `cycleStatus` only counted a pause written after that event, read the cycle as running and not paused, Continue answered `already_running` and `resumeCycle` refused, while the workflow held on the pause signal until its time box. `cycleStatus` now matches the pause row to the open cycle by the `workflowId` in its details (the time test stays for rows without one). Test: `cycle.test.ts` "a pause pressed while the orchestrator is still planning…" (fails before the fix). Until the api is deployed, Pause then Resume on the pill unsticks such a cycle (the second pause row is dated after `cycle.started`).

Our team has been alerted, and nothing was charged to your credits." (the billing sentence's shape), the details carry `billedTo`, and `notePlanFailure` logs `alert: "model_credential"` for the operator; a business on its own key keeps the Settings › Keys sentence. Found when the mobile preview (and Commutair's Inbox) blamed a missing business key for this morning's bad platform key. Tests in `plan-failure.test.ts`. - 2026-11-07: **Automated mode no longer answers an agent's approval for the founder.** Automated mode's Inbox loop (`autoDecideBlocking`, apps/orchestrator `activities/holiday.ts`) took the Approve option of an approval's note (`openApprovalItem`, a gated tool call such as a spend above the approval line): the note's options cost 0, so the spend cap never held it, and its "spending **£X**" wording asked only for `spend_above_threshold`, which Automated mode grants. `repos.inbox.decide` then closed the note without resolving the `approvals` row, so the founder was no longer asked about a spend over their line (Settings › What the team can do: "It still asks you before spending over £X") and the waiting session heard nothing until its approval timed out. `foundersOwnReason` now returns `APPROVAL_IS_YOURS` ("an agent's call waits for your approval") for a note with Approve and Reject options (`isApprovalOption` from @foundmill/db, replacing the orchestrator's copy of the ids) or any note an approval is linked to (`repos.approvals.forInboxItem`), beside the sign-off and goal checks, so Automated mode and vacation skip it (counted in `queuedGated`, listed in the vacation report with that reason). Vacation already refused notes with the approval options (`vacationVerdict`); it now also refuses a note an approval is linked to, and the report counts each such note once, as the approval it answers. Test: `first-days.test.ts` "Automated mode and vacation never answer an approval" (failed before the fix: Automated mode answered both notes; with the cap null and with a cap above the amount). Orchestrator: 71 files, 647 tests pass; typecheck clean; lint has the two existing warnings.

(2) Commit's domain list says when this browser received it ("Checked 2:50 PM"), never the registry's or the mock's own stamp ("Checked 9:00 AM" at 2:50 PM); a failed call stamps nothing, a retry that answered does. ## 2026-11-09 — Phase 3, part 4: the Chief of Staff on every page answers what's happening, what's blocking and why

One "Ask <name>" control on every business page, next to "Explain this page": the Chief of Staff's avatar and name under the reader's naming preference, opening a small popover with "What's happening?", "What's blocking us?", "Why am I seeing this?" and "Ask something else…". An answer is two to four plain sentences from the same facts as Today, with no model call, shown as the Chief of Staff speaking (entity mentions are EntityLinks, projected numbers keep Forecast/Estimate), with "Continue in chat" (the existing agent desk, question as the first message), a skeleton while loading and Try again on error. Hidden when the business has no Chief of Staff.
- **Words (shared).** `packages/shared/src/chief-answers.ts`: `chiefAnswer(question, facts, page)` (pure), `chiefPageKind`, `chiefPagePurpose`, `CHIEF_QUESTION_LABELS`, `AGENT_STOP_REASON_WORDS` (the shell's Resume copy uses it too). It reuses Today's sentence builders; `goalValueText` and `goalPaceState` moved from `apps/web/components/today/today-view.ts` to `packages/shared/src/goals.ts` (re-exported there).
- **API.** `chiefOfStaff.who` and `chiefOfStaff.answer` (`apps/api/src/routers/chief-of-staff.ts`, `services/chief-of-staff.ts`): Today's own reads (`needsYouFor`, `weekFactsFor` now taking an optional cycle status, `goalView`, `cycleStatus`), plus running work with its agent, work waiting on other work (`task_edges`), the stop and vacation states. Same section walls as `today.get`; business-scoped.
- **Design system.** `AskChiefOfStaff` (`packages/dugout/src/components/shell/`), story `Shell/AskChiefOfStaff`; `AppShell` gains `pageActions` (tab row, breadcrumb row, or a thin `page-actions-row` for a page with neither).
- **Web.** `components/shell/AskChief.tsx` (host: `chiefOfStaff.who`, the answer query, page context from the path, the area and the registered Explainer's plain purpose, the desk), `ask-chief.ts` (pure helpers), mounted by `BusinessShell`.
- **Tests.** shared `chief-answers.test.ts`; api `chief-of-staff.test.ts` (answers, walls, other-business refusal, no usage cost); dugout `AskChiefOfStaff.test.tsx` (keyboard, Escape and focus, states, placement in all three rows); web `ask-chief.test.ts`, `ask-chief-host.test.tsx`; Playwright `e2e/ask-chief-of-staff.spec.ts` (Northwind: Today, an area tab, Needs you, the three answers, keyboard, Continue in chat, Ask something else) with today, areas, explain-page, shell, shell-chrome, keyboard-a11y and no-clip re-run. ## 2026-11-10 — Phase 3, part 6 (review fixes): the sample's roll-forward never collides, never moves people's rows

Security review of PR #74 (DECISIONS 2026-11-10 "The sample's roll-forward: only the sample's own rows").
- **Sample list (M2).** Migration 0074 (edited in place, not on main yet) adds `demo_sample_rows` (business_id, table_name, row_ids; tenancy read-only for app roles; schema `src/schema/demo.ts`; cross-tenant spec in `rls/tables.ts`; left out of the export). The seed records every row it wrote (`recordSampleRows`, before each `seed.demo`); a roll moves only those, and takes off the list a sample row changed since the last roll (updated_at newer than its `settledAt`). A seed older than the list gets one at its first roll (rows created before its entry). `ROLL_EXCLUDED_TABLES` adds usage_ledger, billing_checkouts, credentials, webhooks, suppression_list, posts, community_members, saved_table_views, dashboard_layouts and the list itself.
- **Collisions (M1, L4).** Unique dates still park a century ahead; a sample row whose landing key another row holds (the nightly agent rollup's day, a channel engine's day; null keys compared as equal) is dropped, the writer's row wins; the move back uses the same ownership filter; a unique index with an expression over a moved date leaves its table alone, and any other key violation rolls back that table alone (a savepoint per table), named in the result and the audit entry. The roll and `deleteDemoBusinesses` share one advisory lock per demo business (`lockDemoBusiness`).
- **Trigger (L1).** `fm_set_updated_at` keeps the written updated_at only when `fm.demo_roll_forward` holds the row's business id (or the businesses row's own id) and the role is not authenticated or anon.
- **Boot and refresh (L2, L3).** The workflow reconciler waits for the boot roll at most `DEMO_ROLL_BOOT_WAIT_MS` (10 s); `putNorthwindFirst` runs inside `rollDemoForward` after any move; the api refreshes the journey's daily rows itself and retries a failed refresh on every pass; `DEMO_ROLL_FORWARD=off` turns the roll off (packages/shared env, `.env.example`, ARCHITECTURE §3).
- **Sample marker (L5 and CI).** `sampleMarker` needs `isDemo === true` in both branches; `makeBusinessContext` passes the business to `accessFor`. The marker is now a caption under the business name (KitBar `businessBadge`, the name on one line beside it) and its line joins the switcher's tooltip under "Switch business" (`businessBadgeLine`, BusinessSwitcher `tooltipLine`): the name never clips at 1280 and 1440 (no-clip, shell-chrome), the search keeps its placeholder at 1440, and hovering the switcher says "Switch business" (businesses.spec).
- **Flake.** `live.spec.ts` "Find on Foundmill" typed into the server-rendered input before hydration; it now waits for `settled` (also the visibility and Start a community tests) and checks the typed value.
- **Tests.** db `seed/__tests__/roll-forward-ownership.test.ts` (the list; the nightly rollup and a channel day written before four rolls, no wedge, writer's rows kept, far-future sentinel kept; a person's note, task and resolved approval unmoved across three rolls while the sample moves 13 days; billing and posts untouched; backfill for an older seed; expression-index skip and key-violation rollback), `roll-forward.test.ts` (0074 trigger: other business, old 'on', authenticated owner all stamped now()); api `sample-numbers.test.ts` (backfill result, refresh retried, off switch); web `business-shell.test.ts` (no marker on a non-demo business with an example-source membership); dugout `AppShell.test.tsx` (the caption under the name), `BusinessSwitcher.test.tsx` (one tooltip with both lines), KitBar story `ExampleFrame`. Playwright (local stack on other ports): businesses, example-business, live, no-clip (1280 and 1440), shell-chrome and today, 25 passed.
- **Not done.** No concurrency test of the shared lock (PGlite is one connection). A sample table without updated_at cannot tell a person changed one of its rows. A table skipped by the guard falls behind by that roll's days.


## 2026-11-11 — Thesis review and rebuild roadmap (report only, no product change)

`docs/THESIS_REVIEW.md`: a review of the whole platform against the founder's thesis (people move from being employed to building something of their own, and would rather build with others, so founders finding each other and becoming cofounders is the core loop; the user is a young first-time founder; the employment worry stays subtext).
- **Findings.** The product is a console for one experienced founder alone. The people side (connections, messages, communities) is built but switched off, a person is visible only as the owner of a committed public business, there is no invitation, request to join or cofounder, and the free plan has one seat. Every route is blocked under 1280 px, sign-up is closed to a list, and no email reaches a user except the sign-in link. The one real outside action an agent can take is a commit and a pull request on a repository the founder already has; email, payments, web research and channel results are mock in production, and a mock result can read as a real one.
- **Roadmap.** Seven phases with work packages, files and done-when lines: decide and tell the truth; a real first outcome that travels (a public project page); reach and the person; build together; finding each other; coming back; the inside re-cut for a first-timer; economics and reach.
- **Open.** Eleven decisions for the founder (section 14), among them the "Never build mobile layouts" rule, the words for people, free seats and open sign-up. No rule in CLAUDE.md changed.
- **Method.** Read from code at `0904088`; nothing was run (no installed dependencies); production settings from `docs/DEPLOY.md`, not from the live services.

## 2026-11-11 — Rebuild, phase 0, package 0.7 (first part): security prerequisites for live email and payments

A read-only re-check of SEC-9, 10, 11, 14 and 16 found none fully fixed and one new high finding. Fixed here (SECURITY_REVIEW "Re-check and fixes", DECISIONS same date):
- **N1 (high).** `repos.legal.findContractByDocRef` matches the whole request id, never a substring; a signature callback that names no signable contract writes nothing (`apps/ingest/src/contracts.ts`, `IngestResult.skipped`). Tests: `apps/ingest` contracts (one character, a prefix, a middle slice, a host name and a pattern sign nothing; the whole id signs).
- **SEC-10.** `ConnectorWebhook.accountOf` on Stripe, Shopify and GitHub; ingest's `accountMismatch` answers 422 `business_mismatch` for a delivery that names another account than the business connected. Tests: staging-webhooks (GitHub wrong installation refused, right one accepted on retry; Shopify, Stripe through the platform, Stripe on the founder's own key).
- **SEC-11.** SendGrid inbound token compared in constant time; live webhooks keep no replay guard of their own, so a vendor's retry after a refusal is accepted.
- **SEC-16.** `buildUrl` refuses a whole URL that is not https on the connector's own host (new `packages/connectors/src/__tests__/http.test.ts`); the Shopify health check validates the stored shop domain.
- **SEC-9.** `loadEnv` refuses a production process with `APP_ENV` unset, empty or development (`packages/shared/src/env.test.ts`).
- **Verified.** Vitest: connectors 744, ingest 80, shared env 9, db repos 429, api legal-lifecycle 14; tsc on shared, db, connectors, ingest, api and gateway; eslint on the changed files.
- **Not done.** SEC-14, N2, N3, N4, the gateway's development-secret fallback and the Dropbox Sign vendor confirmation. No workspace-wide typecheck or Playwright run for this part.

## 2026-11-11 — Leaderboards removed (founder's decision, THESIS_REVIEW §14 item 9)

- **Web.** The `/leaderboards` routes and screens, the sidebar and palette entries, the auth redirect prefix; Commit and Settings copy no longer promise leaderboards.
- **Widgets and design system.** The status strip's "Leaderboard position" tile (five tiles in every mode) and the `league_table` widget; a stored layout or setting that still names them is read without a reset (tests in `packages/widgets`).

## 2026-11-11 — Rebuild, phase 0, package 0.7 (second part): SEC-14, the api stops calling vendors directly

In mock mode the api still answers from the mocks itself, so the golden path needs no gateway.

- **Independent security review** (verdict "fix before merge"; fixed here): Tink's first step failed in live mode on an empty credential; Tink's second step trusted a user id from the request; a connect could pair a new credential with the previous account's settings if the api's write after the gateway's failed (the gateway now writes the settings with the credential); the Anthropic key's health check was refused; a gateway running a registry's mock while the api expected it live showed sample companies as real.
- **Verified.** Workspace typecheck; vitest: gateway 254 (full), api 988 (full, before the review fixes) and the four api files the fixes touch (35), connectors 762; scripts tests 138; eslint, banned-terms, us-english.
- **Open.** One token secret signs every service's tokens, so any service holding it can call these routes as the api (N4; a separate api-only secret needs a new Railway variable, not done). The api still holds the vault key to seal a founder's Anthropic key. No api test feeds a simulated registry answer through the gateway double.

## 2026-11-11 — Rebuild, phase 1, package 1.5 (first part): a Foundmill sending address for every project

Founder's decision: Foundmill gives a project an address to send email from, with a daily cap and the founder's approval for outside contact.
- **Address.** `project_mail` (migration 0078): one fixed local part per project (the page address, else the slug, with a suffix if taken), never changed once used, never reused after a project is deleted (`retired_mail_local_parts`); role words and the product's name are refused. Sent as "‹project› via Foundmill". An operator raises a project's cap or pauses it in the database; no founder or agent can.
- **Sending.** Built-in connector `platform_mail` (real and mock), one tool `send_mail`, one recipient, plain text and simple HTML, on the platform's SendGrid key held by the gateway only. Guards in order: the existing Cedar rules (outside contact asks, suppression, untrusted recipients), the feature configured, the project not on its own SendGrid, content, suppression, address not paused, the project's daily cap (default 50) and the deployment's ceiling (default 2,000), both refused when the counters are not shared (Redis down). `mail.sent` (with `simulated`) through the outbox. A real project on a mock deployment is refused, never simulated.
- **Coming back.** Inbound Parse at one ingest endpoint: the project from the envelope's local part, replies counted only when SPF or DKIM passes for the sender's own domain, quoted text and the footer ignored when looking for "stop", typed signals only (no body stored). Delivery events bound by custom args the gateway signs (business, message, recipient); bounces, spam reports and unsubscribes suppress. Every message has an unsubscribe link (a confirm page, then POST).
- **Channel Board.** Email is available for every project once the address really sends.
- **Security.** An independent review found nine issues before merge (replies would have unsubscribed their senders; spoofed senders; caps failing open without Redis; address reuse; impersonation; simulated sends; refunds on ambiguous failures; a development signing key; hardening, including the founder's own SendGrid connector falling back to the platform key). All fixed with tests.
- **Verified.** Workspace typecheck after the merge; vitest after the merge: api 997, gateway 273, grants contract 52; the build's runs: ingest 98, connectors 777, sandbox 128, shared 628, db 1923 and RLS 1330, policy 224, orchestrator 665; Playwright `platform-mail.spec.ts` (8) and `settings-plain.spec.ts` (16) against the local stack.
- **Off until configured.** Nothing changes in production until `PLATFORM_MAIL_DOMAIN` and the SendGrid setup in `docs/DEPLOY.md` §5.7 are done.
- **Not done.** Email from Foundmill to a person (invitations, "a decision is waiting"); founders' own SendGrid webhook keys (N2 for that path); a postal address in the footer (marketing law) and domain warm-up.

## 2026-11-11 — Rebuild, phase 1, package 1.6 (first part): "Follow this project" on the public page, with double opt-in

- **Visitor.** On `/p/<slug>` (from 390 px, without JavaScript too): "Follow ‹project›", an email, a required consent box with the exact sentence, then "Check your email to confirm. ‹project› will only write to you after you do." The same answer for every case, so the form reveals nothing about who already follows.
- **Confirmation.** One email from the project's Foundmill address (package 1.5) through a gateway service route; a signed link (7 days) to a confirm page in ingest. Confirming records the consent, sets the lawful basis only if the contact had none, and writes `page.followed`, which is a real outcome. Without platform mail configured no email goes out and the signup stays "Not confirmed yet".
- **Founder.** A switch "Let people follow this project" (on by default for new pages); "N people follow this project" counts confirmed followers only; followers appear in Customers as "Follower (ma***@example.com)" with source "Public page" and "Agreed to email on ‹date›"; an unconfirmed follower cannot be emailed by an agent. Every unsubscribe, bounce or spam report withdraws the consent.
- **Limits, in order.** Per caller 120 a minute; one a day per page and address; three confirmation emails a day per address across all projects and one per project per 30 days; unknown or closed pages only against their own ceiling; then the page's 60 an hour and 300 a day and the site's 600 a minute. Confirmations count against the project's mail cap. Email keys are HMACs.
- **Security.** An independent review found consent claimed for addresses nobody proved they own, budgets drained by repeats and unknown pages, unsubscribes not withdrawing consent, and smaller items; all fixed with tests before merge.
- **Verified.** Workspace typecheck after the merge; vitest after the merge: api 1013, gateway 279, ingest 105; the build's runs: db 1938 and RLS 1345, connectors 780, shared 634; Playwright `public-page.spec.ts` and `platform-mail.spec.ts` (10) against the local stack (the confirm page itself is covered by gateway and ingest tests: the e2e stack runs no gateway or ingest).
- **For the founder of Foundmill.** A Vercel Firewall rate-limit rule on POST `/p/*` (docs/DEPLOY.md §2): until it exists every visitor reaches the api through the web's shared addresses, so there is no per-visitor limit.
- **Not done.** Payment links (the rest of 1.6); "Not confirmed yet" in the Leads table; a daily Inbox summary of new followers.

## 2026-11-11 — Rebuild, packages 1.5 (second part) and 5.1 (first part): email from Foundmill to a person

The first email a user gets from Foundmill beyond the sign-in link: "‹project› needs your answer", when a note that needs that person has waited a day unopened.
- **Path.** Gateway-only connector `platform_notify` (real and mock; never an agent tool) on the platform's SendGrid key, from `hello@` the platform mail domain; route `POST /v1/notify/email` (the api's service token only, ids only: the gateway reads the address from the account itself and re-checks the business, membership, Inbox access, the preference and the note). The api's hourly pass picks: unread notes that need an answer, 1 to 7 days old, members who can read the Inbox, not samples or example visitors, email turned on.
- **Limits.** Once per note, ever (an event and a Redis marker in the gateway); one email per person per project per day and three a day in all; its own deployment ceiling (`PLATFORM_NOTIFY_DAILY_CEILING`, default 1,000), and none while project mail is above 75% of its own. Fails closed without Redis.
- **Content.** The note's subject as that person's Inbox shows it (Finance and CRM walls applied, the redaction now shared in `@foundmill/db/inbox-redaction`); every link, domain and address in team- or founder-written text replaced by "(link)" or "(address)", after NFKC; the only link is into the app.
- **Person's control.** Settings › Preferences › Email ("Email me when something has waited a day for my answer", on by default); a signed turn-off link in every email (a confirm page, then POST).
- **Security.** An independent review found walls leaking into the email, bypassable link stripping, the shared ceiling drained by reminders and "once per note" enforced only in the api; all fixed with tests before merge.
- **Verified.** Workspace typecheck after the merge; vitest after the merge: api 1019, gateway 292; the build's runs: ingest 109, connectors 785, shared 661, events 76, db 1944 and RLS 1345; Playwright `preferences.spec.ts` (11) against the local stack.
- **Not done.** Quiet hours; a weekly summary; email for invitations and people (phase 3). Prose with no space after a dot ("Node.js") is cut as a link in these emails.

## 2026-11-12 — Rebuild, phase 2, package 2.2 (the Google half): Continue with Google on the web

Founder's decision (Nov 12): Google sign-in on the web only, for now; no Apple; the phone app keeps the email code.
- **Sign-in page.** "Continue with Google" above the email link (which is unchanged), Supabase OAuth through the same `/auth/callback` and `next` handling as the email link; `/login` works from 390 px (`SMALL_SCREEN_PREFIXES`). Shown only with `NEXT_PUBLIC_AUTH_GOOGLE=on`, and only after the page has checked that Supabase has Google turned on, so it is never a dead end. Dugout `GoogleSignInButton` (Google's light button and unmodified "G").
- **api.** A Google sign-in counts as a confirmed email when `amr` has `oauth`, `app_metadata.providers` is Google (and email) only, and `user_metadata.email_verified` is true; anonymous, other providers and password-only sessions are refused. The account stays the token's `sub`; Supabase links a Google identity to an existing user only for a verified address. The allowlist gate is unchanged and applies to every request. The api reads Supabase's `mailer_autoconfirm` every 10 minutes and, while it is on, counts no session as confirmed (fails closed). A recent sign-in counts only sign-in methods, never a token refresh.
- **Security.** An independent review said "ok to merge" and asked for the fail-closed check, the password refusal and the sign-in-time rule before Google is switched on; all done with tests.
- **Verified.** Workspace typecheck and lint; vitest api 1062, web 1273, dugout 1150; banned-terms, us-english, plain-language gate; scripts tests 138; Playwright `login.spec.ts` at 1280 and 390 px with the flag off (7) and on (9), and the login and callback tests in three other specs (20). A real Google round trip against the live Supabase project was not run.
- **Founder setup** (`docs/DEPLOY.md` §3 and §4): `SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` on the api service; a Google OAuth client (Web) with the Supabase callback; Google turned on in Supabase; narrowed redirect URLs; "Confirm email" kept on; `NEXT_PUBLIC_AUTH_GOOGLE=on` on Vercel Production.
- **Open.** stream and collab admit by the allowlisted email without the confirmed-email rule; re-signing in through Google proves only the same browser; the invite-only refusal page still asks for a larger window on a phone; `supabase/config.toml` still lists the wide redirect URLs.

## 2026-11-11 — Retention, part 1: measurement and the return loop

The founder asked for retention built in from the first visit and chose to start with measurement and the return loop (decisions recorded in DECISIONS 2026-11-11 "Retention plan").
- **Measurement.** Migration `0083_founder_return.sql` (renumbered from 0076 after #79, then after Team call's 0082): `founder_presence` (last ping, session start, the end of the previous session) and `founder_visit_days` (one row per person, business and business-calendar day), both the person's own rows under RLS. `packages/shared/src/founder-return.ts` defines a session (30-minute gap), activation (both sign-offs and a first Continue within 24 hours of commit) and a habit (3 return days in the week after commit). `founder.presence.ping` / `get` (apps/api) write `founder.session_started` (via direct, email or browser notification). The web shell pings on open, every 5 minutes and on becoming visible (`lib/presence.ts`), and strips `?via=&n=` from the address. `pnpm --filter @foundmill/db report:retention` prints activation and habit for a commit range.
- **Founder email.** `founder_notifications` (sent or skipped, dedupe key per founder, the one-click answer's token hash only; the stored copy keeps no live link). A new service-only connector `founder_mail` (SendGrid on the platform key, real and mock) behind `POST /v1/founder-mail/send` in the gateway (orchestrator and api only; audit row without address or content). `account_preferences` gains `email_notifications` (beside #75's push `notifications`), the brief's hour and zone, browser notifications and the pause.
- **Notifier.** Schedule `founder-notifications` (every 5 minutes, `activities/notify.ts`, pure rules in `notify/rules.ts`, emails in `notify/render.ts`): decisions waiting 30 minutes since the founder left, wins, milestones, a run the founder started and left, used-up credits, the morning brief at their hour and the weekly review on Monday. Never while the founder is in the app, nothing older than a day or seen already, at most 4 event emails a day, owners and admins only, section walls kept (no Inbox, no email; walled from Customers or Money, no subjects). Event `founder.notification_sent`.
- **One-click answers.** `/a/<token>` (public) shows what the answer does; its button calls `email.action.answer`, which re-checks the token (once, 48 hours), the founder (owner or admin with Inbox write, allowlist), the note and the option (`oneClickOption`: recommended, reversible, free; never an approval or sign-off), compiles and evaluates the plan (`oneClickPlanAllowed`: internal actions only, allowed, no hard gate) and decides as that founder. `/email/pause` and the RFC 8058 `/email/unsubscribe` pause every email through a signed token.
- **In the app.** Settings › Preferences › Email (`founder.email.*`: switches, brief hour, browser notifications, pause, latest emails). Today's **Since you were last here** (`today.sinceLastVisit`, dugout `SinceLastVisit` with story). The tab title starts with the Needs you count ("(2) Today · …"). Opt-in browser notifications for a paying customer or a reached goal while the tab is hidden.
- **Overnight shift.** Commit's switch is now **Overnight shift**, on by default: Autopilot with the daily cadence at about 2 AM in the founder's zone (`overnightUtcHour`), one round of work a night.
- **Tests.** shared founder-return, email-links, autopilot; events catalogue; db RLS `founder-return.test.ts`, account preferences, presence repo; api founder-presence, email-links, since-last-visit, overnight-shift; orchestrator notify-rules, notify-sweep, schedules; gateway founder-mail; connectors founder_mail; web presence, browser-notifications, email-answer, since-last-visit, business-title, redirects, naming, CommitForm; dugout SinceLastVisit. Playwright `return-loop.spec.ts` (new) and `commit`, `preferences`, `server-path`, `titles-not-found`, `today` pass on the PGlite stack (titles now allow the count prefix).
- **Not done.** `server-path.spec.ts` "a last business that no longer opens is forgotten" fails here with and without this change (the URL matcher sees the first redirect). Daily streak, early milestones, the idea box on the landing page, Google sign-in and the code sign-in are the next parts. The one-click page and the pause page use the desktop brand frame, so on a phone they ask for a larger window (see DECISIONS).

## 2026-11-12 — Retention, part 2: Autopilot's own limits, phone pages for founder email, the daily streak

Founder, 2026-11-12: "Split the limits, allow phone pages, then do the streak" (DECISIONS 2026-11-12).
- **Autopilot's own limits.** Migration `0084_autopilot_own_limits.sql` (renumbered from 0077 after #79, then after Team call's 0082): `autopilot_own_max_cycles_per_day`, `autopilot_own_spend_ceiling_minor`. They count only the cycles Autopilot starts on its own schedule (`ownAutopilotToday`: `autopilot.cycle_started` with no `runId`, and what those cycles cost), never a press of Continue or its run's rounds; `decideAutopilot` waits on them after the shared limits, and `decideRun` reads the shared ones only. A cycle Autopilot starts on its own carries the rest of its ceiling as `budgetCapMinor` (patch `autopilot-own-budget`); the cycle stops starting work at it and hands each session its reserve as `spendCapMinor`, a new session budget limit `cycle`. Settings › What the team can do › Autopilot has "Its own cycles per day" and "Its own spend per day (Estimate)"; `autopilot.changed` carries them. Commit's overnight shift sets 1 cycle and $0.15 a night.
- **Phone pages.** `/a/<token>` and `/email/pause` render at any width (`lib/phone-pages.ts`, `DesktopOnly`, `BrandFrame allowNarrow`); everything else still asks for 1280 px. CLAUDE.md and DESIGN record the exception.
- **Daily streak.** `packages/shared/src/streak.ts` (`dailyStreak`, `streakWords`, `vacationDayKeys`, `STREAK_MILESTONES`): days in a row from `founder_visit_days`, vacation days frozen, today never breaking a streak before it ends. `today.streak` (the caller's own days, RLS). Dugout `StreakStrip` (story "Today/StreakStrip") on Today. The morning brief adds "You are on a 4-day streak. Open Today to make it 5." from 2 days on.
- **Tests.** shared autopilot (own limits, overnight preset) and streak; db `autopilot-own.test.ts`, autopilot facts; orchestrator businessCycle (a capped cycle, an uncapped one), session budget, notify rules; api overnight-shift (own limits set and cleared), autopilot, streak; web autopilot draft and section, desktop-only (the phone exception), today-view; dugout StreakStrip and client boundaries. Playwright `return-loop.spec.ts` gains the phone check at 390 px and the streak on Today.

## 2026-11-12 — Retention, part 3: one sender for every email

Founder decisions 2026-11-12 (DECISIONS "One sender for every email from Foundmill"); rebased onto main after #79.
- **Migrations** are `0083_founder_return.sql` and `0084_autopilot_own_limits.sql` (after Team call's 0082, journal `when` later than its); 0083 no longer adds `email_notifications`, and `founder_notifications.kind` takes the seven `EMAIL_NOTIFICATION_KINDS`.
- **One sender.** `POST /v1/notify/email` (gateway) now sends every kind, asked by the api (waiting decisions) and the orchestrator (the rest) with ids only; it checks the pause, applies the 30-minute rule for a note that holds up the team's work (`holdingUpWork`) and the 24-hour rule otherwise, counts the per-project and per-person limits for every kind but the briefs (`LIMITED_EMAIL_KINDS`), builds the words (`decisionWaitingEmail`, `retentionEmail` in the new `packages/shared/src/notify-words.ts`), offers the one-click answer, and keeps the record on `founder_notifications`. Facts are read by `loadNotifyFacts` (packages/db). New refusals: `paused`, `present`, `nothing_new`, `not_started`.
- **Deleted.** The `founder_mail` connector, `apps/gateway/src/founder-mail.ts` and `/v1/founder-mail/send`, the `FOUNDER_EMAIL_FROM*` env and `FOUNDER_MAIL_LIVE_KEY`, the orchestrator's `sendFounderMail` and `notify/render.ts`, the event `founder.notification_sent`, `lib/phone-pages.ts` and `BrandFrame allowNarrow`.
- **Orchestrator.** `notify/rules.ts` plans only what is due (kind, ids, dedupe key); `activities/notify.ts` asks the gateway (`notifyEmail` on its client) and records lasting refusals as skipped. `notifyRecipients` no longer reads addresses.
- **api.** The notify pass runs every 5 minutes and takes notes from 30 minutes old; `sentSince` counts only the limited kinds.
- **Web.** One Settings › Preferences › Email section (`EmailNotificationsSection`): a switch per kind, the brief's hour, browser notifications, the pause, the latest emails. `/a/<token>` and `/email/pause` are in `SMALL_SCREEN_PREFIXES`, and CLAUDE.md and DESIGN list them. The off page names the kind (`notifyOffBody`).
- **Tests.** gateway notify-email (the 30-minute rule, the other kinds, the brief outside the limit, the stored copy); api notify-email-pass (the same rules), email-links; orchestrator notify-rules and notify-sweep (rewritten); shared notify-words (moved from the orchestrator), founder-return; events catalogue; connectors grants; ingest notify-off; web preferences-tab, desktop-only, login-form.

## 2026-11-12 — Account-scoped events: a private, person-level event store

Every event belonged to a project (`events.business_id` and `outbox.business_id` NOT NULL, streams keyed by business, every member reading the rows), so a fact about a person had nowhere to go without showing that person to a project's members. The founder decided on Nov 12, 2026 to build the person-level store before the network features need it. Contract: docs/ARCHITECTURE.md §4 (0085), §5 "Account events", §12. Decisions: docs/DECISIONS.md "Account events".

- **One catalog, two scopes** (packages/shared, packages/events). `EVENT_TYPES` gains `profile.published` and `profile.unpublished` at its end; `ACCOUNT_EVENT_TYPES`, `AccountEventType`, `BusinessEventType`, `EVENT_SCOPES`, `isAccountEventType`, `eventScopeOf`. Every catalog declaration carries `scope`; `declareEvent` is business, `declareAccountEvent` is account and checks the payload schema as the catalog loads. `PlatformEvent`, `WriteEventInput`, `buildEvent`, `writeEvent` take business-scoped types only (compiler and runtime); the wrappers in apps/api, apps/composer, apps/gateway, apps/orchestrator and packages/connectors follow with one token each.
- **Storage** (packages/db, migration `0085_account_events.sql`, journal idx 71): `account_events` and `account_outbox`, keyed by `user_id` with cascade from `auth.users`; own-row SELECT for `authenticated` on six columns only; no client write; the queue service role only; `fm_account_events_guard` (no account id in a payload, no UPDATE) and `account_events_actor_own`. Drizzle schema in `schema/governance.ts`, `repos.accountEvents` (read side), `repos.events.write` / `writeMany` refuse account-scoped types. `outbox` is not altered; `events` gains one check (the review entry below).
- **Write path** (packages/events): `writeAccountEvent`, `writeAccountEvents`, `buildAccountEvent` (`outbox/account-writer.ts`); the two-person rule in `account-payload.ts`; the envelope and parsers in `account-event.ts`. `runAccountOutboxRelay` / `createAccountOutboxRelay` / `deleteAccountStream` (`outbox/account-relay.ts`) publish to `ae:{userId}` and record `attempts`, `last_error` and `next_attempt_at`; the polling loop both relays share moved to `outbox/relay-loop.ts`. `consumeAccountEvents`, `decodeAccountEntry` and the `deadLetterRecord` option in `consume.ts`. Keys in `keys.ts` (`accountStreamKey`, `ALL_ACCOUNT_STREAMS_PATTERN`, `ACCOUNT_STREAM_RETENTION_MS`, `ACCOUNT_RELAY_DEAD_LETTER_KEY`).
- **Relay wiring** (apps/api `services/relay.ts`): the supervisor starts the account relay beside the business relay once Redis answers, wakes both after a mutation and stops both.
- **Stream wall** (apps/stream): channel `ae:<userId>` (`accountChannel`, kind `account`), open to that account only; `viewAccountEvent` holds every entry to the channel's account and sends `id, userId, type, occurredAt, payload`; an account-scoped type on a business stream reaches nobody; `EVENT_WALLS` files the business-scoped types. The `u:` signals are unchanged.
- **The first two events** (apps/api `services/person-profile.ts`): `founder.publicProfile.publish` writes `profile.published` and `unpublish` writes `profile.unpublished`, in the same transaction, once per change of state, payload `{ handle }`. An address that is, or holds, an id is refused (`handleLooksLikeAnId`).
- **Verified** (Nov 12, 2026, on this branch):

Also apps/ingest 12 files, 109 tests. No failures.

## 2026-11-12 — Account events: fixes from the security review

The review found no blocker; three fixes and four hardenings were made on the same branch before the pull request. Decisions: docs/DECISIONS.md "Account events: fixes from the security review".

- **Account-scoped types at the database** (migration `0085_account_events.sql`, edited before it was ever applied): `fm_account_event_types()` is the one SQL list; `events_not_account_scoped` on `events` (the one change to that table) and `account_events_type_account_scoped` on `account_events`. A test holds the list equal to `ACCOUNT_EVENT_TYPES`; a member's own insert of `profile.published` into `events` is refused.
- **An account id in any spelling**: the trigger (`fm_account_id_text`, every run of 32 or more letters and digits looked up at every position) and the writer (`accountIdText`, `looksLikeAnId`, hex-only comparison for the people the fact is about); the actor exactly `{ kind, id }`, a system actor a lowercase word. ARCHITECTURE says what is enforced and what cannot be.
- **Handles and take-down** (apps/api): an address that holds an id is refused at save and at publish and is never suggested; `unpublish` builds the event first, writes it in a savepoint, and takes the profile down whatever happens to the event.
- **Hardening**: empty search path and qualified names in the trigger and helpers; refusals and parse failures carry rule codes, issue codes and depths, never a key or a value (`AccountRuleError`, `AccountEventParseError`, `issueReason`); consumer dead letters keep a reason code, the event id and type (`deadLetterRecord`, `accountDeadLetterRecord`). `account_events.updated_at` stays because `migrations.test.ts` requires it of every table but `audit_log`.
- **Verified** (Nov 12, 2026, after the fixes):
  - `pnpm -w turbo typecheck --continue --force`: 25 of 25 tasks, 0 cached.
  - eslint on the changed files: 0 errors, 0 warnings.
  - vitest, whole suites: packages/events 13 files, 122 tests; packages/db 84 files passed and 1 skipped, 2,057 tests passed and 13 skipped (RLS specs included); packages/shared 46 files, 687 tests; apps/api 124 files, 1,088 tests passed and 3 skipped; apps/stream 13 files, 96 tests. No failures.
  - Tests added or widened: packages/db `rls/account-events.test.ts` (22: every spelling of an id in a payload and in an actor, the actor's exact shape, the helpers' search path and grants, the SQL list, the insert into `events` as a member, an owner, the service role and the owner connection) and `migration-0085.test.ts` (one added constraint, a planted row removed); packages/events `account-payload.test.ts` (17), `account-writer.test.ts` (11), `account-store.test.ts` (12: an email-shaped key through the relay, the dead letter's exact fields), `event-scope.test.ts` (6); apps/api `account-events.test.ts` (12: an address that holds an id, take-down after a change of address while public, take-down when the event cannot be built, take-down when its insert fails).
  - `node scripts/banned-terms.mjs`, `node scripts/us-english.mjs`, `node scripts/plain-language.mjs --gate --summary`: pass.
- **Not done.** The follow-ups recorded in DECISIONS (hashed idempotency keys, a caller for `deleteAccountStream`, an alert for a row that fails forever, ordering after a transient failure, the record-id oracle, one run on real Postgres or Supabase). The id rule for addresses is in the api only: `founder_profiles` has no check constraint for it. Not rerun: packages/connectors, apps/gateway, apps/composer, apps/orchestrator (their code did not change in these fixes; the forced typecheck covers them), apps/web's full suite, Playwright, Storybook, evals.

The supervisor's option `accountRelay` turns it on for tests. Why: DECISIONS, same date (production's pool lost connections from the deploy that started the second poller).

## 2026-11-12 — The api's pool: a gate on request transactions

The api restarted every five minutes after #86 because one request of 8 to 10 reads took the whole pool (DECISIONS "The api's pool filled by one request").
- **Gate.** apps/api `services/tx-gate.ts`: `createTransactionGate`, `forRequest(db, signal)`, `gateLimitsFor(poolMax)`; wired in `createApiDeps` (`deps.txGate`) and `createContextFactory` (each request's `ctx.db`); `/health` reports `transactions`. One request holds 4 transactions at a time, all requests the pool less 4; a closed request starts no more; a wait is capped at 15 s.
- **Pool size.** `API_DB_POOL_MAX` (default 10, unchanged); DEPLOY.md "Database connections" has the arithmetic against `max_connections`.
- **Today.** `today.visit` (one transaction for "Since you were last here" and the streak); the web's Today uses it.
- **Tests.** api `tx-gate.test.ts`: a model pool of 10 where three batches of ten starve the watchdog's probe without the gate, and never do with it; early closes; nested transactions; the capped wait; and through the app, a batch of ten opening ten transactions at once without the gate, four with it, six for two batches, nothing left after closed requests. `streak.test.ts` covers `today.visit`.
- **Not done.** The pool itself is not raised (Postgres's connection limit comes first). The postgres.js slot leak behind the stuck pool is not reproduced; it needs a real Postgres.

## 2026-11-12 — Connectors on the founder's own key, first part: how a project appears in AI assistants and search

- **Founder rule.** A connector the founder connects with their own key runs on the founder's own vendor account, always; never a Foundmill platform key, no developer apps, no vendors' own MCP servers; anything outward or costly asks first. In CLAUDE.md ("Always") and docs/DECISIONS.md.
- **Six connectors,** each real and mock with contract tests, a health check on the founder's key, its own rate limits and a row in Settings › Integrations (from the catalog): `openai`, `gemini`, `perplexity` (`ask_panel`: one question, the answer and its sources), `serpapi` (`search_google`: Google's results and AI Overview), `firecrawl` (`scrape_page`: a page as a crawler reads it), `indexnow` (`submit_urls`: tell search engines an address changed; asks first). What comes back from the web or another assistant is untrusted content.
- **IndexNow for a Foundmill-hosted page.** Connect with no site address and the project's public page gets its key file at `/p/<slug>/<key>.txt`; the key speaks for that page only.
- **Agents.** GEO Engine: the three panels (restored from PLANNED_GRANTS.md), search results, page reads, IndexNow. SEO Engine: search results, page reads, IndexNow. Both say which assistants a figure comes from and report one that is not connected as not connected.
- **Evals.** Golden task `channel_geo.connected_assistants`; injection case `web.assistant_panel_answer_override`. Not run against a live model in this change (the suites' structure tests pass).
- **Security review** (docs/DECISIONS.md): no P1; four P2 fixed (the deployment's own host as a "site of your own", hosted submissions limited to the page, telling search engines asks first, daily limits cut).
- **Verified.** Workspace typecheck (25 of 25); lint with no errors; connectors 970, gateway 327, api 1080, web 1322, dugout 1193, agents 439, orchestrator 665, shared 697, models 292, policy 224, evals 96; db `pages-indexnow` and `project-pages` 28; Playwright `public-page.spec.ts` 8 of 8 against the local stack; banned-terms and US English scans clean.
- **Open.** No connector has called its vendor for real (no keys here; each has an env-gated live suite). Whether search engines accept a hosted page's own address with a key file under `/p/<slug>/` needs one check on a real deployment. No daily estimated-spend ceiling across calls, and no deployment-wide ceiling on hosted IndexNow submissions. GEO and SEO agents now read untrusted content as the quarantined reader's digest (they hold an outward tool), so a panel answer's sources do not reach them. The Settings › Integrations rows were not looked at in a browser. Second part: `apollo`, `vercel`, `posthog`, `resend`; `ayrshare` is dropped.

## 2026-11-12 — A home for a person with no project (THESIS_REVIEW 2.4, first part)

A signed-in person with a profile and no project lands on `/home`, a real screen, instead of being sent into the studio. Decisions: docs/DECISIONS.md "A home for a person with no project".

- **Route** (apps/web `app/home/`): `/home` reads the viewer (`loadStudioViewer`, which now also carries `liveMode`), sends a person without a profile to /onboarding and a person with a project to it, and otherwise renders `PersonHome`. The signed-in index (`app/start/page.tsx`) sends a person with no project to `/home` instead of `/new`; `/home` joins `isFounderPath` (redirects.ts) and `SMALL_SCREEN_PREFIXES` (DesktopOnly.tsx).
- **Screen** (`components/person-home/PersonHome.tsx`): "Welcome, <name>"; "Start something" (primary, to /new) with "Find a project or people" beside it as an offer that says the person can do all of it on their own; the find card links to /network when the people layer is open (`features.liveMode`) and says "Opening soon" otherwise. "Waiting for you" (requests to connect, unread messages, community invitations from `live.summary`) and "Pick up where you left off" (concepts in progress, `idea.list`, three) appear only when there is something. No suggestions of people anywhere on the screen.
- **Small screens**: `StudioFrame` takes `smallScreen` (renders at every width, becomes a container, the viewer's email folds away below `page-wide`); the home is one column on a phone and two from `page-wide`, every link at least `tap-target` high.
- **Verified** (Nov 12, 2026, on this branch):
  - apps/web typecheck and eslint on the changed files: 0 errors.
  - vitest: `components/person-home` (new, 8), `components/new`, `components/providers`, `app/__tests__`, `lib`: 582 tests, no failures.
  - Playwright `e2e/person-home.spec.ts` (new) with the onboarding project: 8 of 8 (`/` lands on /home; two columns at 1280; at 390 px no larger-window ask, no sideways scroll, 44 px targets, one column; Start something opens the doors).
  - `node scripts/banned-terms.mjs`, `node scripts/us-english.mjs`, `node scripts/plain-language.mjs`: pass, no new hits.
- **Not done.** The `(live)` layout (/network, /communities, /profiles) still sends a person with no project to /new: those pages need a business shell today, and the change belongs with the network session (asked, Nov 12). Until it lands, "Find a project or people" on the home reaches /network only for a person who has a project. Onboarding still continues to /new after the profile is saved.
